### ARTICLE 17 - CALL BACK TIME

Section 1. Call Back Pay.
Except as modified in Sections 2-4 of this Article, an employee who is called back to
work outside the employee's regular shift, will receive overtime compensation in
accordance with the Overtime Article of this Agreement for hours actually worked; but in
no event will the employee be paid less than four (4) hours at the straight time rate of
pay or two and sixty-seven one hundredths (2.67) hours of compensatory time accrued
(CTA).

Section 2. Telecommuting.
If an employee is called to work and such work is conducted from the employee’s home
or alternate work site, the employee shall be paid only for the time actually engaged in
the Agency’s business. If such work results in an overtime situation as described by the
employee’s telecommuting agreement, the overtime shall be paid at the applicable
overtime rate.

Section 3. Off-Duty Telephone Calls/Text Messages.
An employee who responds to a telephone call or text message outside of their
schedule hours that is directly from Agency management or authorized by Agency

2023-2025 DOC Security Plus               36                   Collective Bargaining Agreement
management will be compensated at the appropriate rate of pay for no less than thirty
(30) minutes or actual time whichever is greater. However, the employee will not
receive additional compensation if the employee receives multiple telephone calls or
texts during the same thirty (30) minute period. This subsection shall not apply to
overtime, which is essentially a continuation of the scheduled work shift. This
subsection shall not apply to telephone calls or text messages where Agency
management directs the employee to report for work, which then engages Section 1 of
this Article instead of this Section. This section shall not apply to employees assigned
to on-call duty or standby duty status or when the Agency calls or texts an employee to
work overtime.

Section 4.
This Article shall not apply when phone calls or texts are a result of employee oversight
(i.e. talking home necessary keys, equipment necessary at the Institution etc.) or when
the Agency calls or texts the employee to share information not requiring call back or
responding to questions.
                                                                                    REV: 2019, 2021


### ARTICLE 18 - REPORTING PAY

An employee who is scheduled and reports to work and there is no work available may
be excused from duty, but shall be paid at their regular rate for the shift of work
scheduled. Work scheduled in this context does not include work hours assigned or
volunteered for outside the employee’s regular schedule.

### ARTICLE 19 – STANDBY DUTY/ON-CALL DUTY

On-call and standby duty will be assigned on an equitable basis among employees who
are qualified to do the work. If the work assigned includes the duties of acting
supervisor, management will determine who is qualified. When on-call or standby duty
is assigned, on-call or standby pay will be in effect. When an on-call or standby duty is
assigned the affected employee will be notified of the effective pay status. Assignment
will be made in writing unless time and circumstance prevent prior written notice.

Section 1. Standby Duty.
a.    An employee shall be on standby duty when required to be available for work
      outside their normal working hours, and subject to restrictions consistent with the
      FLSA which would prevent the employee from using the time while on standby
      duty effectively for the employee’s own purposes.

b.     Compensation for standby duty shall be at an FLSA-eligible employee’s straight
       time rate of pay or for FLSA-exempt employees hour-for-hour compensatory time
       off. Overtime hours shall be at the appropriate overtime pay rate pursuant to
       Article 15.

2023-2025 DOC Security Plus              37                  Collective Bargaining Agreement
Section 2. On-Call Duty.
a.    Employees shall be paid one (1) hour of pay at the regular straight time rate for
      each six (6) hours of assigned on-call duty. Employees who are assigned on-call
      duty for less than six (6) hours shall be paid on a pro-rated basis.

b.     An employee shall be assigned on-call duty when specifically required to be
       available for work outside their working hours and not subject to restrictions
       which would prevent the employee from using the time while on call effectively
       for the employee’s own purposes.

c.     No employee is eligible for any premium pay compensation while on on-call duty
       except as expressly stated in this Article.

d.     On-call duty time shall not be counted as time worked in the computation of
       overtime hours worked but on-call pay shall be included in the calculation of the
       overtime rate of pay.

Section 3.
An employee shall not be on standby duty or on-call duty once they actually
commences performing assigned duties and receives the appropriate rate of pay for
time worked.

### ARTICLE 20 - WORK OUT OF CLASSIFICATION

Section 1.
When an employee is assigned for a limited period to perform the duties of a position at
a higher level classification for at least five (5) consecutive work days, or more, or forty
(40) consecutive straight time hours or more, the employee shall be compensated for all
hours worked beginning from the first day of the assignment at a rate which is five
percent (5%) above the employee’s base pay rate or the first step of the higher range,
whichever is greater.

Section 2. Work Out of Classification Pending Reclassification
a.    When the Agency receives approval from DAS CHRO that a reclassification
      upward is appropriate, the Agency will conduct an internal pay assessment to
      determine the amount of pay the employee will receive while performing the
      duties of the higher classification. The Work Out of Classification Pending
      Upward Reclassification differential will be a dollar amount, which will not be less
      than the next higher step in the higher classification. The Agency will pay work
      out-of-classification pending reclassification effective the first of the month
      following the date the Agency receives the request.

b.     The internal pay assessment shall be conducted using those performing work of
       comparable character in the higher classification.



2023-2025 DOC Security Plus               38                   Collective Bargaining Agreement
c.     The differential shall place the employee on the next higher step in the higher
       classification or on an appropriate higher step as determined by the internal pay
       assessment, whichever is greater.
       1.      If, prior to the submission for reclassification, the employee was receiving
### Article 20 Work Out of Classification pay of the five percent (5%) in the
               newly requested classification for twelve (12) calendar months or more,
               that differential will be combined with the current salary rate when
               calculating the next higher step of the new salary range.

       2.      If, prior to the submission for reclassification, the employee was receiving
### Article 20 Work Out of Classification pay of five percent (5%) for less than
               twelve (12) calendar months, that differential will not be included in the
               salary placement calculation identified in Article 20, Section 2.

       3.      For all of the situations above, while pending permanent financing, the
               employee will continue to receive scheduled step increases and cost of
               living adjustments. With each increase, the differential amount the
               employee is receiving will be recalculated to reflect the next higher step in
               the new salary.

       4.      Employees at the top step of their current job classification will have the
               differential adjusted to the next step in the higher salary range annually on
               their Benefit Service Date (BSD).

       5.      Where the Agency is awaiting DAS CHRO approval of the reclassification
               request, an affected employee may request the differential be withheld in
               order to prevent an overpayment situation. In such cases, and where
               DAS CHRO approval of the reclassification request is obtained, the
               Agency shall make the approval retroactive to the first of the month
               following the reclassification request to the Agency.

d.     The Agency will designate the reason for the work out-of-classification in the
       State Human Resources Information System (HRIS) as Work Out-of-
       Classification – Pending Reclassification.

e.     The employee will continue to receive the differential until the position receives
       final legislative budgetary approval or until the higher-level duties are removed.

f.     For the rest of the reclassification process, refer to Article 46 – Reclassification
       Procedure of this Agreement.

Section 3.
An employee performing duties out of classification for training or developmental
purposes shall be informed in writing of the purpose and length of the assignment
during which there shall be no extra pay for the work. A copy of the notice shall be
placed in the employee's file. Assignment to training to developmental opportunities
shall be voluntary.




2023-2025 DOC Security Plus               39                   Collective Bargaining Agreement
Section 4.
Assignments of work out of classification shall not be made in a manner which will
subvert or circumvent the administration of this Article. This higher class work will be
entered into the employee's personnel file and shall be used for annual performance
appraisals and will be taken into consideration by supervisors during promotional merit
ratings.

Section 5.
Job Rotation is the performance, by mutual agreement, by an employee, of a different
work assignment, on a non-permanent basis, for an agreed-to period of time. A job
rotation may trigger work out of class pay if the assignment is to a classification which is
in a higher salary range.

Section 6.
When the Agency chooses to offer a work out of classification assignment, it shall be
assigned through an open competitive process when the duration is expected to last six
(6) months or longer. The Agency will post the opportunity on its electronic bulletin
board in the Agency public folders for no less than ten (10) calendar days. If the
Agency chooses to continue the work out of classification assignment beyond one (1)
calendar year, the Agency shall re-post the opportunity for no less than ten (10)
calendar days.
                                                                          REV: 2015, 2017, 2019,2023


### ARTICLE 21 - LEADWORK DIFFERENTIAL

Section 1.
Leadwork differential shall be defined as a differential for employees who have been
formally assigned by their supervisor, in writing, "leadwork" duties over two (2) or more
employees in their classification or salary range for ten (10) consecutive calendar days
or longer. Leadwork is where, on a recurring daily basis, while performing essentially
the same duties as the workers led, the employee has been directed to perform
substantially all of the following functions: to orient new employees, if appropriate;
assign and reassign tasks to accomplish prescribed work efficiently; give direction to
workers concerning work procedures; transmit established standards of performance to
workers; review work of employees for conformance to standards; and provide informal
assessment of workers' performance to the supervisor.

Section 2.
The differential shall be five percent (5%) beginning from the first day the duties were
formally assigned in writing for the full period of the assignment.




2023-2025 DOC Security Plus               40                   Collective Bargaining Agreement
Section 3.
Leadwork differential shall not be computed at the rate of time and one-half (1-1/2) for
the time worked in an overtime or holiday work situation, or to effect a "pyramiding" of
work out of classification payment.

Section 4.
Leadwork differential shall not apply for voluntary training and development purposes
which are mutually agreed to in writing between the supervisor and the employee.

Section 5.
The employees who believes they are performing the duties of a leadworker as defined
in Section 1, may request a review of the duties as follows:
       a.     The employee shall notify their supervisor and appropriate Personnel
              Officer, in writing for a review.

       b.      The supervisor, on behalf of the Agency, will respond to the employee in
               writing, within fifteen (15) calendar days from the date of notification.

       c.      If it is the Agency's determination that the leadwork duties were assigned,
               the leadwork differential will be effective the date the employee notified the
               supervisor.

       d.      If the Agency determines that the duties are not leadworker or wishes to
               remove the duties, the employee will be notified as noted in "b" of this
               Article.

Section 6.
When the Agency chooses to make a leadwork assignment or chooses to rotate a
current leadwork assignment to another employee, the Agency shall use a competitive
process to assign a qualified employee. The Agency will email employees in the work
unit where the assignment is available of the opportunity and employees will be able to
apply through a memorandum of interest. The Agency will allow no less than ten (10)
calendar days for employees to submit their interest through this process.

### ARTICLE 22 - TRAVEL/MOVING ALLOWANCES

Section 1.
Travel and Moving Allowances will be reimbursed in accordance with the Department of
Administrative Services Accounting Division Policy.

Section 2.
If the per diem rates change, as reflected in the Department of Administrative Services
Accounting Division Policy, the rates for the Article will likewise be adjusted.



2023-2025 DOC Security Plus                41                   Collective Bargaining Agreement
Section 3. Reimbursement of Expenses Incurred in Rescinded Transfer.
An employee who is given a written notice of transfer that is later rescinded shall be
compensated by the Agency for all expenses incurred which are under section 1 of this
Article. The employee shall furnish the Agency with normally required receipts of
expenses claimed when requesting such compensation. (From Article 52, Section 3)

Section 4. Travel Time on Authorized Agency Business.
When the employee is required by the Agency to travel, the actual travel time shall be
considered time worked. Where required travel is outside an employee’s regular work
hours (excluding normal commuting time), the Agency may temporarily modify the
employee’s weekly schedule without daily overtime or schedule change penalty. Where
such schedule modification still results in the need for additional work hours, the
employee shall be paid the appropriate rate of pay for all time worked over forty (40)
hours in that workweek.

Section 5.
Employees required to travel outside their normal worksite to perform work shall be
subject to the Statewide Travel Policy (OAM 40.10.00).
                                                                                           REV: 2023


### ARTICLE 23 - EMERGENCIES

Section 1.
a.    During periods of bona fide emergency, provisions of this contract regarding work
      assignments and scheduling, job posting, and overtime scheduling may be
      temporarily suspended by the Agency as required for the duration of the
      emergency up to a maximum of thirty (30) calendar days. Should a declared
      emergency be expected to continue beyond thirty (30) calendar days, the
      Employer will provide notice to the Union and an opportunity to meet its request
      to discuss any specifics, modifications, or ongoing temporary suspensions of the
      above aspect of this Agreement.
b.    The Parties acknowledge that the notification of the emergency and/or the length
      of a declared emergency does not waive their rights under PECBA or ORS
      243.698.

c.     Appropriate notification of the emergency status will be made to the Union
       Council Representatives and Local 2376 Statewide Elected Officers.

Section 2.
Emergency is defined as an unforeseen circumstance which may threaten the safety
and security of the public, Adults in Custody, employees and/or property. Emergencies
requiring suspension of the contract will be declared as such by either the Governor, the
Agency Head of the Department of Administrative Services or the Agency Head of the
Department of Corrections or as may be delegated to the Deputy Director or Assistant


2023-2025 DOC Security Plus              42                  Collective Bargaining Agreement
Directors. Nothing in this Article is intended to prevent management from taking
immediate action to protect life and safety of the general public, employees, Adults in
Custody or property, recognizing reasonable time and circumstances for notifications
and declarations under this Article.

This Section shall not be used by management to justify suspension of the above
described contract rights to meet the daily operational needs in filling unexpected shift
vacancies due to absences of scheduled staff which occur from time to time.
                                                                                           REV: 2021


### ARTICLE 25 - WORKING CONDITIONS

Section 1. Workweek.
a.    Workweek Defined. The workweek shall begin at 00:00 a.m. Sunday and end at
      12:00 Midnight the following Saturday. All permanent full-time employees in the
      unit shall be scheduled for five (5) shifts of eight (8) hours with two (2)
      consecutive days off within each workweek, or four (4) shifts of ten (10) hours
      with three (3) consecutive days off within each workweek. Saturday and Sunday
      will be considered as consecutive days off within the workweek.

b.     Work Schedule Defined. A work schedule is defined as the time of day and days
       of the week the employee is assigned to work.

c.     Workweek Adjustments. If a variance from subsection a is required in order to
       accomplish the mission of the Agency, the Agency shall notify the Union of the
       reasons for the change prior to its effective date and the Union Subchapter
       President, Local President and Council Representative shall be afforded an
       opportunity to comment and offer alternative suggestions. If the Union feels the
       change is unreasonable, the matter may be processed as a grievance.

d.     Schedule Changes. Employees subject to changes in shift and days off shall be
       given seven (7) days’ notice prior to implementing a schedule change.

e.     Employee Requested Alternate Work Schedules.
       1.   An alternate work schedule shall be defined as any regular and recurring
            schedule that is other than a five (5) day eight (8) hour work schedule.

       2.      An employee may request an alternate work schedule by submitting an
               Agency Alternative Work Schedule Request Form (CD1495) to their
               immediate supervisor. The supervisor shall review the request, consider
               and approve or deny the request based on the criteria on the Alternative
               Work Schedule Request Form (CD1495). If approved, the employee
               waives any overtime or penalty payments that are a result of the employee


2023-2025 DOC Security Plus               43                 Collective Bargaining Agreement
               transitioning into the approved schedule. If the request is denied, the
               supervisor will give reasons for the denial. If requested, the supervisor will
               meet to discuss the reasons for the denial of the employee’s alternate
               work scheduled request. Alternate work schedule requests that are
               denied may be grieved up to step 3 of the grievance procedure.

       3.      Notwithstanding subsection 2 above, posted positions requiring relief and
               any employees who bid for shifts and days off will not be eligible for
               consideration for an alternate work schedule.

       4.      Pursuant to Article 15 - Overtime, overtime for employees working an
               alternate work schedule shall be time worked in excess of the scheduled
               hours in a day or forty (40) hours per week.

       5.      This section shall not apply to Article 25, Section 3(e)(Boiler Operators),
### Article 25 Section 7(f)(P.E.S.T. Work Schedule) and (n)(Health Services:
               RN/Health Services Technicians) of this Article.

       6.      The Agency shall have the right to revoke an employee’s alternate work
               schedule with thirty (30) calendar day written notice to the employee with
               a copy to the local subchapter President if the schedule no longer meets
               operating needs which shall include the reasons for the change. In
               addition, the employee may return to a five (5) day eight (8) hour work
               schedule with seven (7) calendar day written notice to their immediate
               supervisor. In either case, the Agency shall not suffer any overtime or
               other penalty pay liability for moving into or moving out of an alternate
               work schedule.

       7.      Requests for alternate work schedules shall be considered in order of
               application. If more than one (1) employee makes application for an
               alternate work schedule on the same day and both requests cannot be
               approved, preference shall be given to the employee with the most
               bargaining unit seniority in the Agency.

Section 2. Working Hours.
a.    Work Hours Defined. The standard workday shall be a period of twenty-four (24)
      hours containing eight (8) consecutive hours of work interrupted by rest and meal
      periods.

b.     Safety Release: No employee should be required to physically work eighteen
       (18) hours or more in a continuous twenty-four (24) hour period. In the
       unavoidable circumstance that it occurs, then the following guidelines will be
       followed.


2023-2025 DOC Security Plus                44                   Collective Bargaining Agreement
       1.      The employee shall be released from duty by the supervisor as soon as
               feasible.

       2.      No employee shall be expected or required to report back to work for their
               next regularly scheduled shift within an eight (8) hour window of time from
               the time they are released from work.

       3.      The employee shall be given SRT (administrative paid leave) as
               necessary to avoid any decrease in compensation related to the need for
               adequate rest and recuperation time between shifts.

       4.      This provision does not require the Employer to pay for normal time off.

       5.      This provision does not waive mandatory court obligations.


c.     Rest Breaks. Rest periods will consist of two (2), fifteen (15)-minute breaks away
       from the work area. Rest periods preferably will occur between the second (2nd)
       and third (3rd) hours and the fifth (5th) and sixth (6th)hours of the employee's shift.

       In addition to rest breaks in paragraph #1 of this section, and subject to
       conditions outlined in paragraph #3 of this section, employees working a twelve
       (12) hour shift will be provided an additional fifteen (15) minute rest break.

       The Parties recognize that all staff, however, cannot be guaranteed rest breaks
       on a regular and recurring basis. Management agrees to make every reasonable
       effort to allow rest breaks. This paragraph is not intended to change existing
       practices for non-institution employees.

Section 3. Meal Periods.
a.    A meal period shall be scheduled for each employee at approximately the
      mid-period of the workday, as work permits. This period shall begin upon the
      employee being relieved and shall end upon the employee's return to the work
      station.

       Employees who are not relieved from their work assignment and are required to
       remain in their work area while eating, shall have such time counted as time
       worked.

       In no instance shall an employee be required to use a meal period in excess of
       one (1) hour.

b.     Overtime Meals. A reasonable meal period shall be allowed within the confines
       of the regular scheduled shift (after the sixth (6th) hour or during the overtime shift
       up to the second (2nd) hour) when a security plus employee works overtime shifts


2023-2025 DOC Security Plus                45                    Collective Bargaining Agreement
       of at least four (4) hours beyond the employee’s regular shift. The Agency shall
       furnish the meal, and the time shall count as time worked. If an employee is
       called back to work a full four (4) hour overtime shift with less than two (2) hours’
       notice, the employee’s meal will be furnished by the Agency. If a meal can’t be
       provided, a meal ticket will be given to the employee.

c.     If an employee purchases a meal at the Institution during their regular unpaid
       meal period, and during that unpaid meal break Institution management directs
       the employee to respond or return to work before their purchased meal is
       consumed, they shall report the matter to their direct supervisor or OIC on shift
       within two (2) hours of the incident occurring in order to resolve it.

       If there are unresolved issues surrounding a Security Plus unit employee being
       directed by Institution management to respond during their unpaid meal break
       where they have purchased an Institution meal, it will be addressed at the
       Institution’s local Labor/Management committee. Any resolution shall not create
       any new benefit or right for Security Plus staff. Any local resolution will be on a
       case by case basis and shall be Institution specific.

d.     Except as noted in subsection d) and e), employees currently working on an
       Agency approved work schedule that includes an eight (8) hour or ten (10) hour
       daily shift without an unpaid meal period may continue to do so unless revoked
       by the Agency or employee upon seven (7) calendar days prior written notice. If
       revoked, the written notice and the reasons for the change will be provided to the
       employee and the Local’s Subchapter President. If the employee revokes the
       schedule, the Agency shall not suffer any overtime or other premium pay
       penalties.

e.     Bargaining unit employees desiring to work a schedule without an unpaid meal
       period for a period of not less than thirty (30) consecutive calendar days shall
       make the request in writing to the Agency. The Agency shall review the request
       and approve or deny the request subject to operating needs. The Agency’s
       decision shall not be subject to the grievance procedure. The Agency or
       employee may revoke the schedule that has an unpaid meal period upon seven
       (7) calendar days with prior written notice. If either the Agency or employee give
       written notice of wanting to end the work schedule that does not have an unpaid
       meal period, the written notice shall include the reasons for the change. If the
       employee revokes the schedule, the Agency shall not suffer any overtime, shift
       change penalty pay or other premium pay penalties.

f.     An employee who occupies a position as a Boiler Operator in all institutions shall
       be required to work an eight (8) or ten (10) hour shift without an unpaid meal
       period. Except for Food Service Coordinators employed at CRCI and TRCI, all


2023-2025 DOC Security Plus               46                   Collective Bargaining Agreement
       Food Service Coordinators shall be required to work an eight (8) or ten (10) hour
       shift without an unpaid meal period.

g.     This subsection will not abrogate the work schedule options outlined in Letter of
       Agreement #5 (OSP-M) which does not include an unpaid meal period.

h.     Subsections (e) and (f) shall not apply to Institutional RN’s and Health Service
       Technicians.

Section 4. Clean Up Time.
Except in emergencies, employees shall be allowed a reasonable amount of clean up
time during duty hours, prior to meal breaks and completion of shift. Abuses of clean up
time may be subject to disciplinary action.

Section 5. Institution Working Conditions.
Where employees work varying shifts and days, the Agency will meet to discuss the
working conditions with representative(s) for the Union.

Section 6. Time Trades.
Employees may agree to time trades with other employees who are qualified to perform
the duties required in the course of the trade. Such agreement shall be in writing and
signed by the affected employees. Supervisors shall not withhold approval of time
trades without valid cause.

Time trades are voluntary agreements between employees and shall not cause financial
liability to the employer. The employees are individually responsible to ensure that the
time trade occurs as approved. The employee who fails to complete the trade as
approved shall be charged with leave without pay, unless other leave is approved by the
employee’s supervisor.

Employees will submit requests seven (7) days in advance of the trade date. If a trade
is denied a brief explanation shall be provided on the form, which will then be returned
to the employee.

All time trades shall be completed within ninety (90) calendar days or at the end of the
bid cycle, if applicable, whichever comes first.

Initial trial service employees are restricted from time trades the first six (6) months of
employment. Emergent issues may be considered on a case by case basis by working
with their immediate supervisor to secure the approval of a time trade day off. This
Subsection supersedes any other provision in this Agreement on the subject of time
trades for initial trial service employees.



2023-2025 DOC Security Plus              47                   Collective Bargaining Agreement
The notification deadlines and/or the completion period may be extended or reduced at
individual functional units by mutual agreement between the local Union and the
Functional Unit Manager via the Labor/Management meeting process.

Functional Unit Manager may reduce the minimum notification deadline or extend the
completion period at their discretion and with the agreement of the affected employee.

Section 7.
Subject to operating needs of BHS inside of an Institution, the Agency may notify the
Union and employees of the reasons for adopting a new work schedule that may
include shifts that cover evenings/weekends pursuant to conditions outlined in Article 25
Section 1(b) of this Article. Nothing prohibits the Union from making a timely demand to
bargain under ORS 243.698 et.seq. to bargain the proposed work schedule for Qualified
Mental Health Professionals for the affected Institution.

Section 8. Work Schedules (RNs and Health Service Technicians Only)
a.    This Subsection is a temporary pilot to allow the Parties to explore changes to
      work scheduling with the goal of helping improve recruitment and retention in
      Health Services. This pilot will be effective during the 2019-2023 period and
      shall automatically revert back to previous shift/days off bidding that were in
      effect immediately prior to the implementation of this pilot period unless there is
      mutual agreement to continue or revise these provisions.
      a.      This Subsection shall only apply to Coffee Creek Correctional Institution
              (CCCF), and Eastern Oregon Correctional Institution (EOCI) Health
              Services. All other Health Services work scheduling processes for RNs
              and Health Service Technicians at the other Institutions within the
              bargaining unit shall remain unchanged.

b.     This Subsection #1 becomes effective on the next scheduled shift/days off after
       the 2019-2021 State of Oregon/AFSCME Security Plus Agreement goes into
       effect.

c.     Each Institution cited in Subsection (a) shall develop work shifts that includes
       three (3) days off each week on dayshift, swing shift and graveyard shifts. The
       work shifts can be from a combination but not limited to four (4) eight (8) ten (10)
       twelve (12) hours but will have some combination of these hours.

d.     There shall be one (1) shift/days off bid in November of each year that includes
       two (2) periods of six (6) month blocks of time covering the following year that will
       be bid together in order of time in classification seniority. Once a bid has
       occurred, and when a position becomes vacant that the Institution intends to fill
       the Institution will open a solicitation for that bid shift/days off to all employees to
       submit their interest for the vacant position by memo. Employees will have a


2023-2025 DOC Security Plus                48                    Collective Bargaining Agreement
       minimum of seven (7) calendar days to notify the Institution within their interest in
       the shift/days off schedule. The Institution will assign the schedule to the most
       senior employee who submitted. The resulting vacancy will be filled by Institution
       management using the same process above. Any further vacancies that the
       Institution intends to fill can be done with new hires, limited duration or other
       means at management’s discretion. The selected employee will start the new
       schedule on the first following Sunday without any pay penalty.

e.     If there is a conflict between the language in this Subsection and language in any
       Institution specific language, this language shall prevail.
                                                                                              REV: 2023


Section 9. Local Working Conditions.
A.    HEALTH SERVICES
RN’s and Health Services Technicians
      1.     To insure proper staffing levels and adequate health services within
             Institutions, the Parties have agreed to an alternate work schedule for both
             full-time and non-full-time (part-time-percentage) RN’s and Health
             Services Technicians to include twelve (12)-hour shifts.

               Overtime, for the purposes of this Agreement, shall be paid as follows:
               a.    Full-time and part-time (percentage) employees’ hours worked in
                     excess of twelve (12) hours per day will be overtime.

               b.      Full-time and non-full-time (part-time-percentage) employees’ hours
                       worked in excess of forty (40) hours per week will be overtime.

       2.      Full-time RN’s and Health Services Technicians who work an alternate
               work schedule may work three (3) twelve (12)-hour shifts and one (1) four
               (4)-hour shift or a combination of twelve (12) and eight (8) hour shifts for a
               total of forty (40) hours per workweek. Regular or reoccurring time trades
               between two (2) or more employees that seek to cover their four (4) hour
               shift are not considered modifying an employee bid.

               Non-full-time (part-time-percentage) RN’s and Health Services
               Technicians may work a combination of shifts to include twelve (12) hour
               shifts. The Parties agree that if a RN or Health Services Technician is
               absent from work on a scheduled twelve (12)-hour shift, twelve (12) hours
               of accrued leave will be required to cover that absence.

       3.      As written in Article 15, Section 3, shift change penalty pay does not apply
               to employees working an alternate workweek with regular posted shifts
               that may include working more than eight (8) hours in a twenty-four (24)
               hour period.


2023-2025 DOC Security Plus                49                   Collective Bargaining Agreement
       4.      When a holiday occurs on a day the employee would normally be
               scheduled to work but does not, the employee will be allowed to utilize
               other available paid leave for the balance of the holiday off (eight (8) hours
               HO and four (4) hours of VA = twelve (12) hours for the shift). When a
               holiday occurs on a day the employee would normally be scheduled off,
               the employee will receive eight (8) hours of Corrections paid holiday (eight
               (8) hours of pay at the straight time rate).

               Non-full-time employees (part-time percentage) will receive the prorated
               amount of holidays that fall within the employee’s work schedule.
               (Example: Employee who is sixty-four percent (64%) will receive five and
               one-tenth (5.1) hours of the holiday either in holiday (HO) or
               compensatory time straight (earned or paid).

               Employees working the holiday shall be compensated as follows:
                    Holiday occurs on the day an employee is to work (12) hours

                       Eight (8) hours of holiday pay (part of the employee’s regular
                       salary)

                       Four (4) hours of regular pay (part of the employee’s regular salary)

                       Four (4) hours half (½) time (extra pay (half-time (½))

                       Eight (8) hours of holiday pay at time and one-half (1 ½) (extra pay
                       (time and a half 1 ½))

                       Holiday occurs on the day employee is scheduled to work four (4)
                       hours

                       Four (4) hours of holiday pay (part of the employee’s regular salary)

                       Four (4) time and a half (1 ½) (extra pay)

                       Four (4) Corrections paid holiday (extra pay straight time)

       5.      Nothing in this Agreement changes the authority or ability of the Agency to
               change an employee’s work schedule to meet Agency operating needs in
               accordance with applicable provisions of the Collective Bargaining
               Agreement.




2023-2025 DOC Security Plus                 50                   Collective Bargaining Agreement
B.     HEALTH SERVICES
CCCF: Health Services – RN’s and Health Services Technicians
Shift and Day’s Off Bid process (LPN’s and RN’s)
For purpose of shift bidding, seniority is defined as total length of DOC represented
service. Health Service Techs (LPN’s) and Registered nurses (RN’s) shall bid
combined based upon their Agency seniority for a twelve (12) month period of time.
Health Services (CCCF) management will post the shift bid fourteen (14) days prior to
the bid. The Shift and Day’s Off bid will take place no less than thirty (30) days before
the schedule change.
Employees on initial trial service may bid for shift and days off in order of seniority as
defined as DOC Represented time in class. Employees who fail to successfully bid,
who do not apply to bid, will be assigned at the discretion of the Health Service’s
Management.

Bids will address only shift and days off. Bidding will occur every twelve (12) months,
becoming effective on the last Sunday of January. The Medical Services Manager or
designee shall post assignment rotation pattern at least thirty (30) calendar days prior to
the start of the new bid. Management reserves the right to change the assignment
rotation pattern to meet operational needs. Management retains the right to fill positions
as stated in Article 10 of this Collective Bargaining Agreement.

The following definitions and practices are identified for shift and days off bidding:
       a.    New Hires – All employees with the same hire date, will have their
             seniority determined by lottery (i.e., individual employees from each
             affected new hire grouping will draw a number). This will be their
             permanent seniority. (Not to be redrawn each bid cycle.)

       b.
               1.      The Agency will provide and post seniority lists that show each
                       employee state hire dates and total Agency time. Before the bid is
                       implemented, the Union will be provided a copy of the lists at least
                       six (6) weeks before the bid. The Union will review the lists and
                       confirm in writing the Union’s agreement with the accuracy of the
                       lists within two (2) weeks. The Union agrees to update this
                       information as necessary to include new transfers, hires and
                       promotions.

               2.      If an employee’s seniority date has been changed and the
                       employee disputes their seniority date, they shall submit the issue
                       in writing to the Health Services Human Resources Manager within
                       ten (10) calendar days of the date they became aware of the
                       matter. The Health Services Human Resources Manager will



2023-2025 DOC Security Plus                51                  Collective Bargaining Agreement
                       forward a copy to the Local President and Local Oregon AFSCME
                       Council Representatives.

               3.      A committee will be established which shall be composed of three
                       (3) Union appointed from the Security Plus bargaining unit
                       employed in Health Services at the Institution. The Agency shall
                       appoint two (2) management representatives from Health
                       Services/Institution management.

               4.      The committee will deliberate and resolve the dispute. A majority of
                       committee members must vote for a resolution. The committee’s
                       decision will be final and binding on all Parties. Any resolution shall
                       conform to the terms of the agreement. Seniority disputes covered
                       under this Section will not be subject to the grievance procedure.

               5.      The Institution shall not change an employee’s shift/days off so the
                       employee can participate on the committee. The Institution shall
                       not be liable for any overtime as a result of being on the committee.

       c.      All documents for bidding will be published documents and posted,
               emailed or mailed to any staff not present because of time off and/or
               training. The documents showing available shifts and days off will indicate
               credentials necessary to bid for that slot.

       d.      Bids can be submitted in person or by telephone. Submission by e-mail to
               management and a designated Union representative prior to the bidding
               time will be accepted.

       e.      Individuals in a job-share will bid on the seniority of the individuals with the
               highest seniority.

       f.      Each employee will be given a time frame for bidding. If the employee is
               in a position that needs relief, the employee will request the relief from the
               managers at the beginning of the employee’s shift. Management will have
               a relief person to the position just prior to the bid time for the relieved
               employee.

       g.      The Institution will attempt to allow employees in Variable Relief (VR)
               positions to remain on their specific bid shift (Day, Swing or NOC).
               However, due to operational constraints, management reserves the right
               to utilize VR positions as per contract.




2023-2025 DOC Security Plus                 52                   Collective Bargaining Agreement
Once a BID has occurred, and when a position becomes vacant, that the Institution
intends to fill, the Institution will open solicitation for that BID Shift/days off to all
employees to submit their interest by memo. Employees will have seven (7) calendar
days to notify the institution of their interest in the open shift/days off schedule. The
Institution will assign the schedule to the most senior employee who submitted. The
resulting vacancy will be filled by Institution management using the same process
above. Any further vacancies that the Institution intends to fill can be done at
management’s discretion. The selected employee will start the new schedule based on
management’s discretion but no later than ten (10) days once management has filled
the vacancies. Such changes shall occur without any pay penalty.

Employees in the process of training and orientation may bid for an open slot but will not
assume that slot until the orientation and training process is complete. Assignment to
the open slots will be by seniority. Weekly assignments for the upcoming workweek will
be posted via Agency email for nurses to view by the prior Wednesday at 12:00 pm
(noon). Management reserves the right to change employee work assignments to meet
operational needs.

Distribution of Voluntary Overtime.
The primary method for filling available overtime will be through volunteers.
Management retains responsibility but delegates the day to day function of assigning
overtime to the Infirmary RN on shift. Management will normally perform this task while
on site except for the limited circumstances when they are unable to accomplish it in a
timely manner. In these instances the task will be assigned to the Infirmary RN.

When overtime work is needed, management or designee shall first ask Part-Time, Job
Share or Float Pool staff who are available to work on straight time. Secondly, the staff
who have signed up on the Overtime Calendar will be asked if they wish to work the
overtime opportunity. If there are no volunteers from the Overtime Calendar,
management or designee shall ask on-shift staff in order of the mandatory list if they
wish to volunteer for the overtime opportunity. If the employee accepts the overtime
assignment, the employee’s name will be moved to the bottom of the mandatory
overtime list.

Mandatory Overtime.
a.   When management is unable to fill a work assignment through voluntary means,
     mandatory overtime will be used. In such cases, management will designate
     mandatory overtime on a rotation basis to all employees on their regular
     scheduled shift based on the state hire date seniority list by inverse order and the
     date of the last mandatory overtime worked once the list is established.
     Employees who are on voluntary overtime will not be subject to being mandated.
     Employees who are on time trades or have scheduled time trades the next day



2023-2025 DOC Security Plus              53                   Collective Bargaining Agreement
       will only be mandated after all other available employees have already been
       mandated.

b.     When mandatory overtime must be assigned, the Agency shall assign the work
       to the next staff member available on the list.

c.     Movement to the bottom of the list requires an employee to work a minimum of
       two (2) hours of a designated mandatory overtime shift or actually working at
       least two (2) complete voluntary overtime shifts in a seven (7) day time period.

d.     The mandatory overtime list shall be posted electronically and in the Staff Call In
       Book.

e.     All nursing staff may not be selected for mandatory overtime on those days which
       are the employee’s Friday and/or on the day before bidded vacation time.
       However, nursing staff may choose to accept a mandatory overtime assignment
       on their Friday and receive credit for it.

f.     Regardless, the non-biddable staff in Receiving and Discharge and the Infection
       Control Nurse will be exempt from mandatory overtime assignments outside of
       their regularly assigned duties.

g.     Mandatory overtime assignments can be split. It will be the employee’s
       responsibility to find another employee to split the shift with and the mandated
       employee is responsible for coverage of the complete shift. The mandated
       employee shall notify their supervisor or designee of the split overtime shift and
       the other employee’s name and the hours to be worked by both employees.
       Both employees will be rotated to the bottom of the mandatory overtime list by
       working two (2) hours or more.

h.     The mandated employee may select their assignment from all assignments for
       that shift except for those assignments filled by employees who cannot be moved
       due to HR approved restrictions, or non-bid positions.

Section 1. Annual Bid Vacation Process.
a.    Vacations will be bid on the basis of seniority which is based upon the definition
      in Article 32 Section 8 of the Parties Collective Bargaining Agreement.

b.     The annual vacation bid will occur during the first full week of November. A
       Union representative and a Management representative shall be present each
       day.

c.     Management will post a list of employees by seniority including their seniority
       date a minimum of seven (7) days before the bid. The list will also include the


2023-2025 DOC Security Plus              54                   Collective Bargaining Agreement
       assigned time for employees to place their bids in ten (10) minute increments
       during which the employee will place their vacation request. Any employees on
       leave or absent at the time of posting this list and not expected to return prior to
       the vacation bid will be contacted and provided this information in order to ensure
       they may submit their bid should they wish.

d.     If an employee fails to place their bid in person or by phone during their allotted
       time, they may bid at the next available time but they shall not be able to bump
       another employee who has already bid during their allotted time.

e.     Employees will bid vacation utilizing the following matrix:

       Years of State Service at Time of Bid      Number of Weeks Number of Hours
       1.    15 Years or more                     6 weeks          (240 hrs)
       2.    From 10 up to 15 Years               5 weeks          (200 hrs)
       3.    From 5 up to 10 Years                4 weeks          (160 hrs)
       4.    From 3 up to 5 Years                 3 weeks          (120 hrs)
       5.    From 0 up to 3 Years                 2 weeks          (80 hrs)

f.     For the 2023-2025 contract, the changes made in this Section shall be a pilot and
       will automatically revert to the 2021-2023 contract language upon its expiration,
       unless extended by the Parties.         After one (1) year from the date of
       implementation, either party can request to reopen this Section to address any
       problems that arise. There will be one (1) bid process where employees may bid
       consecutive blocks of time, consisting of their regularly scheduled shifts or may
       spread the designated amount of days over different months with the following
       conditions:
       1.     Employees may only bid a maximum of two (2) weeks during the months
              of June, July or August.

       2.      Employees may only bid a maximum of one (1) vacation week during the
               weeks that encompasses the month of March, or the week of
               Thanksgiving or the week of Christmas selecting only one (1) of those
               choices.

       3.      Following the completion of the first bid, a second will take place in order
               of seniority only if open slots remain. During the second, employees may
               bid up to forty (40) additional hours to be taken in complete shifts, as are
               available to bid regardless of the above matrix and if they still have or will
               accrue sufficient vacation time to use at the time it is taken. Under this
               Subsection employees may bid single shifts.

g.     Regardless of the above matrix, employees may only be able to bid based upon
       the hours accrued and what they will earn prior to the time the vacation actually
       occurs. Compensatory Time shall not be used for the purposes of vacation
       bidding.

h.     Prior the vacation bid occurring, a report of the accrued leave banks will be
       produced. There will be a minimum of three (3) employees off during the months


2023-2025 DOC Security Plus                55                   Collective Bargaining Agreement
       of March, July and August and the week of Thanksgiving and the week of
       Christmas. For March, July and August the third (3rd) vacation time off will begin
       the first (1st) full week and continue for thirty (30) calendar days. The remainder
       of the year will have a minimum of two (2) employees off for their bid vacation.

Section 2. Returned Vacation Time.
Bid vacation weeks that are returned at least thirty (30) calendar days prior will be
posted by the Agency within seven (7) working days electronically to the employees via
email. Employees will have five (5) calendar days to send a reply email indicating they
would like to use that time off. At the end of the five (5) calendar days, all email replies
will be placed in a “hat” and a lottery draw will occur with an uninvolved Union member
and a manager.

For the third (3rd) vacation time off during the prime vacation time periods in Section 1(j),
if an employee returns a week during those weeks the first returned week will not utilize
this returned vacation time process and will be used by the facility as it chooses. If no
reply emails are received within the five (5) calendar days period, or the bid vacation
time is returned within less than thirty (30) calendar days in advance of the scheduled
time off, that time will be used by the facility as it chooses.

Section 3. Supplemental Vacation Requests.
Supplemental vacation requests, (requests for time off that are outside of the annual bid
vacation or returned vacation processes), need to be submitted at least one (1) week
after the annual vacation bid and no earlier than sixty (60) calendar days prior to the first
day of any consecutive requested time off and awarded on a first come first served
basis. Leave requests will be allowed for up to two (2) staff off, per twenty-four (24)
hour period. Approval or denial of the submitted request will be made no later than the
twentieth (20th) of the month prior to the requested days. If denied, the reasons for the
denial will be documented in the Human Resource Information System (HRIS).

Section 4.
In all cases, when either the employee or management believes that an employee will
not have enough hours accrued for the hours of vacation scheduled, the vacation will be
cancelled or reduced to match the employee’s accrued hours following discussion with
the employee before the monthly schedule is posted and in order to have sufficient time
to put out that vacation time to other employees through the process described in
Section 2 of this Agreement.

Non Bid Assignments
Non-Bid Assignments (filled by seniority and interest).

The three (3) current positions are:
      (2) Receiving Institutional Nurse Positions
      (1) Infection Control Nurse Position
Non-biddable assignments will be included in the annual review of assignments.

Management reserves the right to exempt bid slots for the purpose of work assignments
that require additional skills, knowledge and licensure. These slots will be filled by

2023-2025 DOC Security Plus               56                    Collective Bargaining Agreement
interested and appropriately credentialed nurses based on an interview process. When
a non-biddable assignment is vacated, it will be announced and appropriately
credentialed interested individuals may apply and interview for the opening. If there is
more than one (1) position within like non-biddable assignments, those individuals will
bid by seniority for shifts/days off during the annual shift/days off bid. Staff will have
access to the list of non-biddable positions.

Time Trades
Employees may agree to time trades with other employees who are qualified to perform
the duties required in the course of the trade. Such agreement shall be in writing and
signed by the affected employees. Supervisors shall not withhold approval of time
trades without valid cause. Time trades are voluntary agreements between employees
and shall not cause financial liability to the Employer. The employees are individually
responsible to ensure that the time trade occurs as approved. The employee who fails
to complete the trade as approved shall be charged with leave without pay, unless other
leave is approved by the employee’s supervisor. Employees will submit requests seven
(7) days in advance of the trade date. If a trade is denied a brief explanation shall be
provided on the form, which will then be returned to the employee. All time trades shall
be completed within a ninety (90)-day time period, or at the end of the bid, whichever is
shorter. If the trade is not approved, Management must provide in writing before the
trade is to occur the reason the trade was declined. The notification deadlines and/or
the completion period may be extended or reduced at individual functional units by
mutual agreement between the local Union and the Functional Unit Manager via the
Labor-Management meeting process. Functional Unit Manager may reduce the
minimum notification deadline or extend the completion period at their discretion and
with the agreement of the affected employee.

Behavioral Health Services (BHS) – Medium and Minimum Facility except Intake
Where management determines that a QMHP work assignment requires working on a
Saturday and/or Sunday, management will first seek a volunteer(s). If there are no
volunteers the least senior regular status employee will be assigned. Seniority shall be
defined as time in classification within the AFSCME Security Plus bargaining unit.

CRCI: Health Services – RN’s and Health Services Technicians
A.    Work Schedule
      Should management determine there is a need to change the work schedule for
      any reason, other than emergent operations, management will discuss with
      AFSCME with the intent of reaching consensus and otherwise reserving the
      Parties rights under PECBA.

B.     Positions
       1.     When a post become vacant it will be offered to each nurse by AFSCME
              time in classification seniority within a week of becoming vacant.



2023-2025 DOC Security Plus              57                   Collective Bargaining Agreement
       2.      Nurses may trade shifts and days off when mutually agreed to by both
               nurses and approved by management which shall not be unreasonably
               denied.

       3.      Management cannot hire a nurse into a specific shift and days off until the
               more senior staff have had the opportunity to request it based on
               AFSCME time in classification seniority.

C.     Vacation.
       1.    Vacation requests will be made the month prior by the 20th of each month.
             If the request is submitted after the 20th, and does not result in overtime, it
             will be approved.

       2.      The State will allow at least one (1) nurse vacation leave per calendar day.

       3.      Vacation will be given on a first come first serve basis. If requests are
               made on the same date, the tie breaker will be state service.

D.     Overtime
1.     Definitions
       a.      Scheduled Overtime. A shift of four (4) hours or more of needed coverage
               that is known more than five (5) days in advance.

       b.      Unscheduled Overtime. A shift of four (4) hours or more of needed
               coverage that is made known in five (5) days or less in advance.

       c.      Mandatory Overtime. Overtime that is needed that day or the following
               shift and nobody has volunteered to work the hours.

2.     Voluntary Overtime Procedure
       a.    Overtime shall be voluntary except during periods of emergency or when
             management is unable to fill a work assignment through voluntary means.

       b.      Overtime shall be assigned in the following order:
               i.    Scheduled overtime shall be posted to allow an opportunity for staff
                     to volunteer. Extra shifts will be offered in the following order:
                     1.     Employees who would earn straight time.

                       2.     Employees who would earn overtime.

               ii.     Voluntary overtime will be given on a first come first serve basis. If
                       requests for overtime are made on the same date, the tie breaker
                       will be AFSCME time in classification seniority.




2023-2025 DOC Security Plus                58                   Collective Bargaining Agreement
               iii.    Once an overtime shift has been assigned to an employee, it can
                       be rescinded and assigned to a straight time employee as long as
                       at least one (1) week notice is provided.

               iv.     For unscheduled overtime management will contact on shift staff
                       first and then off shift staff that have expressed interest in working
                       overtime.

3.     Mandatory Overtime
       a.   If there are no volunteers, management will assign a staff member that is
            currently on shift to work mandatory overtime.

       b.      The qualified employee that has not worked a mandatory overtime shift of
               two (2) hours or more in the longest time frame will be assigned the
               mandatory overtime.

       c.      Health Services Technicians may only be mandated if working with a
               Registered Nurse.

       d.      Trial service employees or employees on their ‘Friday’ prior to a bidded
               vacation will be exempt from being assigned mandatory overtime.

       d.      Management retains the right to fill these positions as stated in Article ten

2.     Overtime.
       a.    Overtime shall be voluntary except during periods of emergency or when
             management is unable to fill a work assignment through voluntary means.


2023-2025 DOC Security Plus                59                   Collective Bargaining Agreement
       b.      In such cases, management will designate mandatory overtime on a
               rotation basis to all employees on each shift based on the union seniority
               list initially by inverse order. Once an employee has successfully

               be placed on the list based on seniority.

       c.      Movement to the bottom of the list requires an employee to work a

       e.      Staff will not be placed in the bucket on their Friday or the day before their

               overtime, immediately prior and contiguous to their next assigned shift,

3.     Vacation Bidding.
       a.    Vacations will be bid on the basis of seniority which is based upon the

               seniority date a minimum of seven (7) days before the bid. The list will

       e.      Employee will bid vacation utilizing the following matrix:

               2.    From 10 up to 15 Years               5 weeks
               3.    From 5 up to 10 Years                4 weeks
               4.    From 3 up to 5 Years                 3 weeks

       h.      Prior to the vacation bid occurring, a report of the accrued leave banks will

       i.      There will be no more than one (1) employee off a day for bid vacation.

EOCI: Health Services – RN’s
1.    For purpose of shift bidding, seniority is defined as Time in Classification with the
      Agency.

2.     Employees on trial service will not be eligible to bid until they have successfully
       completed their trial service or orientation for those employees transferring to the
       institution with regular status, whichever is applicable, not to exceed six (6)
       months, unless trial service has been extended.

3.     Shift Bid Process
       a.     All bargaining unit Registered Nurses are encouraged to develop a
              schedule and submit that schedule to management.

       b.      Management will review all schedules submitted by staff to assure that the
               needs of the Institution are met.

       c.      All Agency approved schedules are then displayed no less than thirty (30)
               days before the bid.

       d.      Each Registered Nurse will then vote on the six (6) month staff schedule
               they prefer.

       e.      The six (6) month staffing schedule that is preferred by the majority of
               nurses will be used for bidding the next six (6) month work schedule.

       f.      If there are no schedules submitted by staff that meets the Institution’s
               needs, the Medical Services Manager or designee shall establish the work
               schedule.

       Bids will address only shift and days off. Bidding will occur every six (6) months,
       becoming effective on the first Sunday of December and June. The Medical
       Services Manager or designee shall post the work schedule for review at least
       thirty (30) days prior to the bid. The shift and days off bid will take place no less
       than thirty (30) days before the schedule change. Bid shifts and days off rotation
       cannot be traded. Employees can bid the same shift for two (2) consecutive bids
       and then are required to bid on a different shift or days off.




2023-2025 DOC Security Plus               62                   Collective Bargaining Agreement
4.     The following definitions and practices are identified for shift and days off bidding.
       a.     New Hires – all employees with the same hire date, will have their
              seniority determined by lottery (i.e., individual employees from each
              affected new hire grouping will draw a number). This will be their
              permanent seniority. (Not to be redrawn each bid cycle.)

       b.      The Agency will post the Agency seniority list. The work schedule for
               bidding and the bidding schedule will be published documents and posted
               in the nurses’ station, emailed or mailed to any staff not present because
               of time-off and/or training.

       c.      Bids can be submitted to the manager and union representative in person,
               by phone, in writing or email. All phone bids will be validated by a union
               represented member and signed by a manager. A union representative
               will be present during the entire bid process.

       d.      To complete the bid process, each nurse will be assigned a 15-minute
               block of time to submit a bid. Employees who fail to successfully bid or
               who choose not to bid, or are hired after the most recent bid, will be
               assigned at the discretion of the Medical Services Manager.

5.     Nothing in this Article changes the authority or ability of the Agency to change
       employee work schedules to meet operational needs consistent with the terms of
       this agreement. Should Agency management decide to redeploy staff, other than
       flex staff, Agency management will first seek qualified volunteers to fill the
       vacancy. If still unable to fill the vacancy, or long term absence, Agency
       management will make changes to the schedule that cause the least amount of
       disruption to the schedule.

6.     When there is a need for staff to work overtime and it cannot be filled by
       voluntary means, the mandatory overtime process will be instituted. In the event
       that overtime is necessary, the following guidelines will be used:
       a.     Monthly schedules will be posted on or about the 18th of the previous
              month.

       b.      Voluntary overtime for the month will be listed on the back of the schedule
               when posted and updated as needed.

       c.      Staff shortage hours are defined as any hours worked to fill staff shortage
               whether unscheduled straight time or overtime.




2023-2025 DOC Security Plus               63                    Collective Bargaining Agreement
       d.      Staff shortage hours in excess of two (2) hours will move the employee to
               the bottom of the mandatory overtime list. All staff are responsible for
               recording staff shortage hours on the mandatory overtime list.

       e.      No staff will be assigned mandatory overtime on their last work day before
               their annual bidded vacation. Staff will not be mandated to work overtime
               during the orientation period.       This does not prevent staff from
               volunteering during their orientation period for overtime that is posted so
               long as the employee has been oriented to the required job duties.

       f.      Staff voluntarily working overtime on their scheduled day off will not be
               mandated to work extra hours immediately following their shift. Staff
               working trades shall not be exempt from mandatory overtime assignment.

7.     When possible, management will provide reasonable unpaid meal period not to
       exceed thirty (30) minutes when an employee works unscheduled voluntary or
       mandatory overtime that is adjacent to the end of their shift and where the
       employee works at least four (4) hours of overtime beyond the employee’s
       regular shift.

8.     Vacation Scheduling.
       a.    Vacations will be bid on the basis of seniority. Seniority shall be defined
             as in Article 32, Section 8 of the Agreement.

       b.      The bid time for vacations shall be from January 1 to December 31 of the
               upcoming year.

       c.      The vacation bidding process shall take place over a two (2) day period
               between the fifteenth (15th) of the October to the fifteenth (15th) of
               November. A Union representative and management representative shall
               be present each day.

       d.      Management shall post a list of employee sin seniority order a minimum of
               seven (7) calendar days before the bidding days. The list will include
               times of individual bids, in ten (10) minute increments during which the
               employee shall place their vacation request.

       e.      Vacation bids may be made by email, in person or by telephone with the
               Union and management representatives present. If the employee fails to
               make their vacation bid by any of the above means, within their allotted
               time frame, they will go the bottom for the list for that day of bidding.




2023-2025 DOC Security Plus               64                  Collective Bargaining Agreement
       f.      Vacation bids are based on earned accrued vacation hours only,
               excluding any other paid leave. Bids may include projected vacation
               hours to be earned by the time of actual vacation time off.

       g.      Bid vacation time may not be traded with another employee.

       h.      If bid vacation hours are returned, the Institution will use the hours as
               needed.

       i.      Requests for vacation leave outside of the vacation bid process will be
               reviewed and approved or denied by the eighteenth (18th) of the preceding
               month, Employees wishing to request vacation leave that would be
               beyond thirty (30) calendar days but less than one (1) year from the date
               of request will be received by management and held until the month
               before the leave is requested. At that time management will approve or
               deny the request. Employees can check the order of their vacation leave
               request by reviewing the notebook in the manager’s office.

Institution Specific Fleece Jackets
Upon request, each Registered Nurse and Health Services Technician will be provided
a fleece jacket to wear during inclement weather conditions. The jacket will be the
Institution’s property and shall be returned upon separation of employment.

1.    To insure proper staffing levels and adequate health services within Institutions,
      the Parties have agreed to an alternate work schedule for both full-time and non-
      full-time (part-time-percentage) RN’s and Health Services technicians, including
      but not limited to eight (8), ten (10), and twelve (12) hour shifts where those
      schedules meet operational needs without incurring extra expense.

2.     Shift and Days off Bidding
       a.     The Institution will establish the work schedule.

       b.      Employees on trial service will not be eligible to bid. Employees
               transferring in from another Institution with regular status will not be
               eligible to bid until orientation is completed.

       c.      For purposes of shift bidding, seniority shall be defined as time in class
               within the Agency adjusted for breaks in service with the AFSCME
               Security Plus Bargaining unit.

       d.      Bids will address only shift and days off. Bid slots cannot be traded.

       e.      The Institution will post the shift bid thirty (30) days prior to the bid. The
               schedule for bidding and the bidding schedule will be published and
               posted in the nurse’s station.

       f.      The Institution will provide employees on unpaid and paid leave the shift
               bid information if they will not be present during the posting period.

       g.      The bid shall take place no later than thirty (30) days prior to the schedule
               change.

       h.      Bids shall become effective on the first Sunday of June and December.
               Upon the signing of the 2013-2015 agreement, the Institution shall
